FILE: frontend/src/types/payload.ts
================================================
// Types for master_index.json and nodes/node_{id}.json produced by export_ui_payload.py

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Shared primitives
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface TimestepEntry {
  t: number;
  prediction: number; // μ (ZINB mean)
  uncertainty: number; // σ (std_dev)
}

export interface PayloadMetadata {
  num_nodes: number;
  num_real_nodes: number;
  num_simulated_nodes: number;
  timesteps: number;
  seq_len: number;
  veh_class: string;
  dir: string;
  start_date: string;
  end_date: string;
  // --- ARTIFACT TWINNING ---
  wandb_run_id?: string;
  git_commit_hash?: string;
  hyperparameters?: any;
}

export interface EdgeEntry {
  source: number;
  target: number;
  attention_sequence: number[];
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// ZINB Calibration types  (Iteration 4)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** One bin of the Probability Integral Transform histogram. */
export interface PITBin {
  bin_center: number; // midpoint of the [0,1] bin (e.g. 0.05, 0.15 …)
  density: number;    // fraction of PIT values in this bin; uniform at ~0.1 when well-calibrated
}

/** One bin of the zero-inflation reliability diagram. */
export interface ZeroInflationBin {
  pi_center: number;               // midpoint of the predicted-π bin
  observed_zero_fraction: number;  // actual fraction of true zeros in this bin
  count: number;                   // number of observations contributing to this bin
}

/** Per-node Two-Extremes missingness bracket. */
export interface TwoExtremes {
  mae_drop_nan: number | null;  // MAE after dropping NaN ground-truth rows; null when none exist
  rmse_drop_nan: number | null;
  mae_fill_nan: number;         // MAE after filling NaN ground-truth rows with 0
  rmse_fill_nan: number;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// master_index.json schema
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface NodeEntry {
  id: number;
  location_name: string;
  lat: number;
  lon: number;
  is_simulated: boolean;
  timeseries: TimestepEntry[];
  semantic_tags?: string[];   // flattened POI/land-use tags; absent in pre-sharding payloads
  two_extremes?: TwoExtremes; // absent in payloads generated before Iteration 4
}

export interface MasterIndex {
  metadata: PayloadMetadata;
  nodes: NodeEntry[];
  edges: EdgeEntry[];
  pit_histogram?: PITBin[];                          // absent in pre-Iteration-4 payloads
  zero_inflation_reliability?: ZeroInflationBin[];   // absent in pre-Iteration-4 payloads
  global_imputation_burden?: number[];
  global_healing_quality?: number[];
  attention_entropy_series?: number[];
  tripwire_audit_trail?: Record<string, any>[];
  alpha_node_leaderboard?: Record<string, any>[];
}

// Backward-compat alias used by MapVisualizer / NodeInspector
export type UIPayload = MasterIndex;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// nodes/node_{id}.json schema  (deep per-node analytics)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface ZINBEntry {
  t: number;
  mu: number;
  theta: number;      // dispersion parameter
  std_dev: number;
  pi: number;         // zero-inflation probability ∈ (0,1) — absent in pre-Iteration-4b payloads
  is_missing: boolean; // true = this timestep was artificially masked before imputation
}

/** One directed GAT attention edge in the localized ego-graph. */
export interface AttentionEdge {
  source_id: number;
  target_id: number;
  direction: "incoming" | "outgoing";
  weight_sequence: number[];  // per-timestep GAT attention weights, length = num_timesteps
  source_lat: number;
  source_lon: number;
  target_lat: number;
  target_lon: number;
}

export interface HopEdge {
  neighbor_id: number;
  direction: "outgoing" | "incoming";
  attention_sequence: number[];
}

export interface NodeDetailPayload {
  id: number;
  location_name: string;
  lat: number;
  lon: number;
  is_simulated: boolean;
  zinb_series: ZINBEntry[];
  ground_truth_series: (number | null)[];
  imputed_series: number[];
  mask_series: boolean[];
  edges_1hop: HopEdge[];
  two_extremes?: TwoExtremes;       // absent in payloads generated before Iteration 4
  ego_graph_edges?: AttentionEdge[]; // absent in payloads generated before Iteration 4b
}

FILE: frontend/tests/payload.test.ts

/**
 * tests/payload.test.ts
 *
 * Schema-integrity tests for the ui_payload.json structure.
 *
 * We use a *mock* payload rather than loading the real 4.8 MB file so tests
 * are fast and network-free.  The mock mirrors the exact structure produced by
 * export_ui_payload.py and typed in src/types/payload.ts.
 */

import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import type { UIPayload, NodeEntry, EdgeEntry } from '@/types/payload';

// ── Mock payload factory ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function makeNode(id: number, isSimulated: boolean, timesteps: number): NodeEntry {
  return {
    id,
    location_name: `Sensor ${id}`,
    lat: 54.97 + id * 0.001,
    lon: -1.61 + id * 0.001,
    is_simulated: isSimulated,
    timeseries: Array.from({ length: timesteps }, (_, t) => ({
      t,
      prediction: Math.random() * 500,
      uncertainty: Math.random() * 0.5,
    })),
    // Phase 1 Analytics Upgrade: imputation series live alongside the forecast.
    // Simulated nodes have no physical sensor → ground_truth is null and the
    // mask is true at every timestep (every input was NaN by construction).
    ground_truth_series: Array.from({ length: timesteps }, () =>
      isSimulated ? null : Math.random() * 100,
    ),
    imputed_series: Array.from({ length: timesteps }, () => Math.random() * 100),
    mask_series: Array.from({ length: timesteps }, () =>
      isSimulated ? true : Math.random() < 0.15,
    ),
  };
}

function makeEdge(source: number, target: number, timesteps: number): EdgeEntry {
  return {
    source,
    target,
    attention_sequence: Array.from({ length: timesteps }, () => Math.random()),
  };
}

function makeMockPayload(
  numRealNodes = 5,
  numSimNodes = 2,
  timesteps = 96,
  numEdges = 10,
): UIPayload {
  const nodes: NodeEntry[] = [
    ...Array.from({ length: numRealNodes }, (_, i) => makeNode(i, false, timesteps)),
    ...Array.from({ length: numSimNodes }, (_, i) =>
      makeNode(numRealNodes + i, true, timesteps),
    ),
  ];

  const edges: EdgeEntry[] = Array.from({ length: numEdges }, (_, i) =>
    makeEdge(i % numRealNodes, (i + 1) % numRealNodes, timesteps),
  );

  return {
    metadata: {
      num_nodes: numRealNodes + numSimNodes,
      num_real_nodes: numRealNodes,
      num_simulated_nodes: numSimNodes,
      timesteps,
      seq_len: 12,
      veh_class: 'cyclist',
      dir: 'from town',
      start_date: '2024-05-10',
      end_date: '2024-05-10',
    },
    nodes,
    edges,
  };
}

// ── Schema integrity tests ────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('UIPayload schema', () => {
  it('metadata.num_nodes equals nodes.length', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 2, 96, 10);
    expect(p.nodes.length).toBe(p.metadata.num_nodes);
  });

  it('metadata.num_real_nodes + metadata.num_simulated_nodes equals num_nodes', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(45, 5, 96, 100);
    expect(p.metadata.num_real_nodes + p.metadata.num_simulated_nodes).toBe(
      p.metadata.num_nodes,
    );
  });

  it('every node has timeseries.length === metadata.timesteps', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 2, 96);
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      expect(node.timeseries.length).toBe(p.metadata.timesteps);
    }
  });

  it('every timeseries entry has a t, prediction, and uncertainty field', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(3, 1, 4);
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      for (const entry of node.timeseries) {
        expect(entry).toHaveProperty('t');
        expect(entry).toHaveProperty('prediction');
        expect(entry).toHaveProperty('uncertainty');
        expect(typeof entry.prediction).toBe('number');
        expect(typeof entry.uncertainty).toBe('number');
      }
    }
  });

  it('timeseries t values are sequential indices 0…timesteps-1', () => {
    const timesteps = 8;
    const p = makeMockPayload(2, 1, timesteps);
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      node.timeseries.forEach((entry, idx) => {
        expect(entry.t).toBe(idx);
      });
    }
  });

  it('every edge has attention_sequence.length === metadata.timesteps', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 0, 96, 20);
    for (const edge of p.edges) {
      expect(edge.attention_sequence.length).toBe(p.metadata.timesteps);
    }
  });

  it('simulated nodes are correctly flagged', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 3, 96);
    const simulated = p.nodes.filter((n) => n.is_simulated);
    const real = p.nodes.filter((n) => !n.is_simulated);
    expect(simulated.length).toBe(p.metadata.num_simulated_nodes);
    expect(real.length).toBe(p.metadata.num_real_nodes);
  });

  it('all node ids are unique', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(10, 5, 4);
    const ids = p.nodes.map((n) => n.id);
    expect(new Set(ids).size).toBe(ids.length);
  });

  it('all attention weights are in [0, 1] (normalised by the export script)', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 0, 10, 50);
    for (const edge of p.edges) {
      for (const w of edge.attention_sequence) {
        expect(w).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0);
        expect(w).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1);
      }
    }
  });

  it('every node has imputed/ground-truth/mask arrays of length timesteps', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(4, 2, 12);
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      expect(node.imputed_series.length).toBe(p.metadata.timesteps);
      expect(node.ground_truth_series.length).toBe(p.metadata.timesteps);
      expect(node.mask_series.length).toBe(p.metadata.timesteps);
    }
  });

  it('simulated nodes carry null ground truth and all-true mask', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(3, 4, 16);
    for (const node of p.nodes.filter((n) => n.is_simulated)) {
      expect(node.ground_truth_series.every((v) => v === null)).toBe(true);
      expect(node.mask_series.every((m) => m === true)).toBe(true);
    }
  });

  it('real-node ground truths are numeric (not null)', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 0, 12);
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      for (const v of node.ground_truth_series) {
        expect(typeof v).toBe('number');
      }
    }
  });

  it('node coordinates are within the Newcastle upon Tyne bounding box', () => {
    // Rough bounding box: lat 54.93–55.05, lon -1.75–-1.48
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 0, 4);
    // Just validate our mock nodes have valid numeric coords (real tests
    // against real data would check the actual bounding box).
    for (const node of p.nodes) {
      expect(typeof node.lat).toBe('number');
      expect(typeof node.lon).toBe('number');
      expect(isNaN(node.lat)).toBe(false);
      expect(isNaN(node.lon)).toBe(false);
    }
  });

  it('contains Artifact Twinning metadata fields', () => {
    const p = makeMockPayload(5, 2, 96);
    // Inject the fields into the mock for testing
    p.metadata.wandb_run_id = 'test-run';
    p.metadata.git_commit_hash = 'abcdef123456';
    p.metadata.hyperparameters = { model: { hidden_dim: 32 } };

    expect(p.metadata).toHaveProperty('wandb_run_id');
    expect(p.metadata).toHaveProperty('git_commit_hash');
    expect(p.metadata).toHaveProperty('hyperparameters');
    expect(p.metadata.wandb_run_id).toBe('test-run');
  });
});

/**
 * tests/slider.test.tsx
 *
 * React Testing Library tests for the TimeSlider component.
 *
 * Strategy: mock the Zustand store so this test is decoupled from real store
 * state and we can assert exactly which action gets dispatched on slider change.
 */

import React from 'react';
import { describe, it, expect, vi, beforeEach } from 'vitest';
import { render, screen, fireEvent } from '@testing-library/react';
import '@testing-library/jest-dom';

// ── Store mock ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// We mock the *module* so TimeSlider consumes our controlled values.

const mockSetTimestep = vi.fn();
const mockTogglePlayback = vi.fn();
const mockIncrementTimestep = vi.fn();

const storeState = {
  currentTimestep: 0,
  maxTimesteps: 95,
  isPlaying: false,
  setTimestep: mockSetTimestep,
  togglePlayback: mockTogglePlayback,
  incrementTimestep: mockIncrementTimestep,
};

vi.mock('@/store/useTimeStore', () => {
  const selector = (fn: (s: typeof storeState) => unknown) => fn(storeState);
  return { default: selector };
});

// ── Helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Import component AFTER mocks are registered.
import TimeSlider from '@/components/TimeSlider';

function renderSlider() {
  return render(<TimeSlider />);
}

beforeEach(() => {
  vi.clearAllMocks();
  storeState.currentTimestep = 0;
  storeState.isPlaying = false;
});

// ── Tests ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('TimeSlider rendering', () => {
  it('renders the time label for timestep 0 as "00:00"', () => {
    renderSlider();
    expect(screen.getByText('00:00')).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it('renders the play button when not playing', () => {
    renderSlider();
    expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /play time animation/i })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it('renders the pause button when playing', () => {
    storeState.isPlaying = true;
    renderSlider();
    expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /pause playback/i })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it('shows the correct t= counter text', () => {
    storeState.currentTimestep = 12;
    renderSlider();
    expect(screen.getByText(/t = 12 \/ 95/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

describe('TimeSlider interactions', () => {
  it('dispatches setTimestep with the new numeric value when the slider changes', () => {
    renderSlider();
    const slider = screen.getByRole('slider', { name: /timestep selector/i });
    fireEvent.change(slider, { target: { value: '24' } });
    expect(mockSetTimestep).toHaveBeenCalledOnce();
    expect(mockSetTimestep).toHaveBeenCalledWith(24);
  });

  it('dispatches togglePlayback when the play button is clicked', () => {
    renderSlider();
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /play time animation/i }));
    expect(mockTogglePlayback).toHaveBeenCalledOnce();
  });

  it('dispatches togglePlayback when the pause button is clicked', () => {
    storeState.isPlaying = true;
    renderSlider();
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /pause playback/i }));
    expect(mockTogglePlayback).toHaveBeenCalledOnce();
  });

  it('slider min/max attributes match maxTimesteps from the store', () => {
    renderSlider();
    const slider = screen.getByRole('slider', { name: /timestep selector/i });
    expect(slider).toHaveAttribute('min', '0');
    expect(slider).toHaveAttribute('max', '95');
  });

  it('slider value reflects currentTimestep from the store', () => {
    storeState.currentTimestep = 48;
    renderSlider();
    const slider = screen.getByRole('slider', { name: /timestep selector/i });
    expect(slider).toHaveAttribute('value', '48');
  });
});

FILE: frontend/tests/store.test.ts

/**
 * tests/store.test.ts
 *
 * Unit tests for useTimeStore (Zustand).
 * Tests run against the real store implementation — no mocking needed.
 * Each test resets the store to a known baseline via setTimestep / setMaxTimesteps
 * so tests are order-independent.
 */

import { describe, it, expect, beforeEach } from 'vitest';
import useTimeStore from '@/store/useTimeStore';

// Reset store state before every test so cases are fully isolated.
beforeEach(() => {
  useTimeStore.setState({
    currentTimestep: 0,
    maxTimesteps: 95,
    isPlaying: false,
    selectedNodeId: null,
    globalAttentionThreshold: 0.5,
  });
});

// ── setTimestep ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('setTimestep', () => {
  it('moves to a valid timestep', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(42);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(42);
  });

  it('clamps to 0 when given a negative value', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(-5);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(0);
  });

  it('clamps to maxTimesteps when given a value beyond the upper bound', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(200);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(95);
  });

  it('accepts exactly 0 (lower boundary)', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(10);
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(0);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(0);
  });

  it('accepts exactly maxTimesteps (upper boundary)', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(95);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(95);
  });
});

// ── setMaxTimesteps ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('setMaxTimesteps', () => {
  it('updates maxTimesteps', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setMaxTimesteps(47);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().maxTimesteps).toBe(47);
  });

  it('setTimestep respects the new max after setMaxTimesteps', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setMaxTimesteps(10);
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(20);
    // Should be clamped to new max of 10
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(10);
  });
});

// ── togglePlayback ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('togglePlayback', () => {
  it('starts as false', () => {
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().isPlaying).toBe(false);
  });

  it('toggles from false → true', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().togglePlayback();
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().isPlaying).toBe(true);
  });

  it('toggles from true → false', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().togglePlayback(); // → true
    useTimeStore.getState().togglePlayback(); // → false
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().isPlaying).toBe(false);
  });
});

// ── incrementTimestep ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('incrementTimestep', () => {
  it('increments by 1', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(5);
    useTimeStore.getState().incrementTimestep();
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(6);
  });

  it('wraps to 0 when at maxTimesteps', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(95);
    useTimeStore.getState().incrementTimestep();
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(0);
  });

  it('does not overshoot maxTimesteps − 1 before wrapping', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(94);
    useTimeStore.getState().incrementTimestep(); // → 95 (not wrapping yet)
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(95);
    useTimeStore.getState().incrementTimestep(); // → wraps to 0
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(0);
  });

  it('wraps correctly with a non-default maxTimesteps', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setMaxTimesteps(3);
    useTimeStore.getState().setTimestep(3);
    useTimeStore.getState().incrementTimestep();
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().currentTimestep).toBe(0);
  });
});

// ── setSelectedNodeId ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('setSelectedNodeId', () => {
  it('starts as null', () => {
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().selectedNodeId).toBeNull();
  });

  it('sets a numeric id', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setSelectedNodeId(42);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().selectedNodeId).toBe(42);
  });

  it('clears back to null', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setSelectedNodeId(7);
    useTimeStore.getState().setSelectedNodeId(null);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().selectedNodeId).toBeNull();
  });
});

// ── setGlobalAttentionThreshold ──────────────────────────────────────────────

describe('setGlobalAttentionThreshold', () => {
  it('defaults to 0.5', () => {
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().globalAttentionThreshold).toBeCloseTo(0.5);
  });

  it('accepts an in-range value', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setGlobalAttentionThreshold(0.4);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().globalAttentionThreshold).toBeCloseTo(0.4);
  });

  it('clamps below 0', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setGlobalAttentionThreshold(-0.5);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().globalAttentionThreshold).toBe(0);
  });

  it('clamps above 1', () => {
    useTimeStore.getState().setGlobalAttentionThreshold(2);
    expect(useTimeStore.getState().globalAttentionThreshold).toBe(1);
  });
});

FILE: notes/scaling_verification.md
================================================
# Scaling Strategy Verification Report

This report summarizes the verification of how data scaling is applied to static and dynamic features in the `ResilientIoTPipeline`.

## 1. Dynamic Features
- **Mechanism**: `StandardScaler` from `sklearn.preprocessing`.
- **Scaling Scope**: **Global (Cross-Node and Cross-Time)**.
- **Implementation**: The dynamic data tensor of shape `[num_times, num_nodes, n_features]` is flattened into `[-1, n_features]` before `fit_transform` is called.
- **Impact**: 
    - **Pros**: Preserves the absolute magnitude differences between different geographical locations and different times.
    - **Cons**: As noted in your query, it is susceptible to extreme outliers. A single sensor reporting an erroneous 10,000% spike would compress the variance of all other sensors.

## 2. Static Features
- **Mechanism**: `StandardScaler`.
- **Scaling Scope**: **Global (Cross-Node)**.
- **Features Scaled**: Currently includes `nearby_poi_count` and `dist_to_nearest_intersection`.
- **Coordinates Verification**: 
    - **Finding**: Latitude and Longitude (coordinates) are **not** currently used as static input features in the `RealIoTDataset`. 
    - **Usage**: They are utilized during the preprocessing phase (`src/preprocess.py`) for spatial joining with weather sensors and fetching POI data, but they are not passed to the model as features.
    - **POI Density**: `nearby_poi_count` acts as the POI density feature and is scaled globally across all nodes.

## 3. Target Scaling (Forecast)
- **Mechanism**: **Unscaled**.
- **Rationale**: The pipeline uses a Zero-Inflated Negative Binomial (ZINB) loss. This loss function requires non-negative integer-like counts. Applying `StandardScaler` to the target would produce signed floats, breaking the ZINB distribution assumptions.

## 4. Local vs. Global Scaling Comparison

| Feature Type | Current Implementation | Effect |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Dynamic** | **Global** | Preserves spatial variance; sensitive to global outliers. |
| **Static** | **Global** | Normalizes metadata across the network; preserves relative "importance" of nodes. |
| **Target** | **None** | Maintains raw count domain for ZINB loss. |

### Verification Details (src/dataset.py)
```python
# Scaling dynamic features (Global)
num_times, num_nodes_d, n_dyn_features = self.dynamic_data.shape
dyn_flat = self.dynamic_data.reshape(-1, n_dyn_features)
self.scaler = StandardScaler()
dyn_scaled = self.scaler.fit_transform(dyn_flat)

# Scaling static features (Global)
stat_np = stat_df.values  # [num_nodes, static_feat_dim]
self.static_scaler = StandardScaler()
stat_np_scaled = self.static_scaler.fit_transform(stat_np)
```

## Recommendations
1. **Coordinate Injection**: If coordinates are meant to be features for the `DynamicGraphLearner`, they need to be explicitly added to the `stat_df` in `src/dataset.py`.
2. **Robust Scaling**: To handle the outlier concern while maintaining global context, consider `RobustScaler` (quantile-based) instead of `StandardScaler`.
3. **Local Scaling Option**: If the model struggles to converge because some nodes are significantly busier than others, a "Local-Global" hybrid (scaling per-node but passing the node's mean/std as static features) might be worth exploring.

FILE: src/analysis.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import argparse
import os
import sys
import yaml
import optuna
import pandas as pd
from typing import Optional, List, Dict, Any

def setup_logger():
    import logging
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format='%(levelname)s: %(message)s')
    return logging.getLogger(__name__)

logger = setup_logger()

def set_nested_value(d: dict, keys: list, value):
    """Recursively sets a value in a nested dictionary."""
    for key in keys[:-1]:
        d = d.setdefault(key, {})
    d[keys[-1]] = value

def list_studies(storage: str):
    """Lists all studies in the specified storage."""
    try:
        summaries = optuna.get_all_study_summaries(storage)
        if not summaries:
            print(f"No studies found in {storage}")
            return

        print(f"\nStudies in {storage}:")
        print(f"{'Name':<40} {'Trials':<10} {'Best Value':<15}")
        print("-" * 65)
        for s in summaries:
            best_value = f"{s.best_trial.value:.6f}" if s.best_trial is not None else "N/A"
            print(f"{s.study_name:<40} {s.n_trials:<10} {best_value:<15}")
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to list studies: {e}")

def analyze_study(storage: str, study_name: str):
    """Performs detailed analysis of a specific study."""
    try:
        study = optuna.load_study(study_name=study_name, storage=storage)
        
        completed_trials = [t for t in study.trials if t.state == optuna.trial.TrialState.COMPLETE]
        
        if not completed_trials:
            print(f"No completed trials found in study '{study_name}'")
            return

        print(f"\n" + "="*60)
        print(f"ANALYSIS: {study_name} ({len(completed_trials)} completed trials)")
        print("="*60)

        # 1. Best Trial & Metadata
        best_trial = study.best_trial
        print(f"\nBEST TRIAL: #{best_trial.number}")
        print(f"Value: {best_trial.value:.6f}")
        
        git_commit = best_trial.user_attrs.get("git_commit", "N/A")
        git_branch = best_trial.user_attrs.get("branch", "N/A")
        print(f"Artifact Twinning (Git): {git_branch} @ {git_commit[:8] if git_commit != 'N/A' else 'N/A'}")
        
        print("\nBest Parameters:")
        for k, v in best_trial.params.items():
            print(f"  - {k}: {v}")

        # 2. Hyperparameter Importance
        if len(completed_trials) > 1:
            try:
                importances = optuna.importance.get_param_importances(study)
                print("\nHyperparameter Importance:")
                for param, importance in importances.items():
                    print(f"  - {param:<30}: {importance:.4f}")
            except Exception as e:
                logger.warning(f"Could not calculate importance: {e}")
        
        print("="*60 + "\n")

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to analyze study: {e}")

def promote_study(storage: str, study_name: str, config_path: str):
    """Updates the config YAML with the best parameters from the study."""
    try:
        study = optuna.load_study(study_name=study_name, storage=storage)
        best_params = study.best_params
        
        if not best_params:
            logger.error("No best parameters found to promote.")
            return

        with open(config_path, 'r') as f:
            config = yaml.safe_load(f)

        print(f"\nPromoting best parameters to {config_path}...")
        for param_name, value in best_params.items():
            keys = param_name.split('.')
            if len(keys) > 1:
                set_nested_value(config, keys, value)
                print(f"  - Updated {param_name} -> {value}")
            else:
                # Handle legacy/top-level parameters if they exist
                # This logic should match src/tune.py mapping
                if param_name in ["lr", "batch_size"]:
                    set_nested_value(config, ["training", param_name], value)
                elif param_name in ["w_forecast", "w_impute"]:
                    set_nested_value(config, ["loss", param_name], value)
                elif param_name in ["sparsity_threshold", "num_layers", "embedding_dim", "hidden_dim"]:
                    set_nested_value(config, ["model", param_name], value)
                elif param_name == "mask_prob":
                    set_nested_value(config, ["data", param_name], value)
                else:
                    config[param_name] = value
                print(f"  - Updated {param_name} -> {value}")

        with open(config_path, 'w') as f:
            yaml.dump(config, f, default_flow_style=False)
        
        logger.info(f"Successfully promoted parameters to {config_path}")

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to promote study: {e}")

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Optuna Study Analysis & Management")
    parser.add_argument("--storage", type=str, default="sqlite:///optuna.db", help="SQLite storage string")
    
    subparsers = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", help="Command to execute")
    
    # List command
    subparsers.add_parser("list", help="List all studies in the database")
    
    # Analyze command
    analyze_parser = subparsers.add_parser("analyze", help="Analyze a specific study")
    analyze_parser.add_argument("study_name", type=str, help="Name of the study to analyze")
    
    # Promote command
    promote_parser = subparsers.add_parser("promote", help="Promote best parameters to a config file")
    promote_parser.add_argument("study_name", type=str, help="Name of the study")
    promote_parser.add_argument("--config", type=str, default="configs/train/train.yaml", help="Path to config file")

    args = parser.parse_args()

    if not os.path.exists(args.storage.replace("sqlite:///", "")):
        # Check if DB file exists before trying to load it
        db_file = args.storage.replace("sqlite:///", "")
        if args.command in ["list", "analyze", "promote"]:
            logger.warning(f"Database file '{db_file}' not found.")
            # If listing, we can just stop. If other commands, they will fail anyway.
            if args.command == "list": return

    if args.command == "list":
        list_studies(args.storage)
    elif args.command == "analyze":
        analyze_study(args.storage, args.study_name)
    elif args.command == "promote":
        promote_study(args.storage, args.study_name, args.config)
    else:
        parser.print_help()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

FILE: src/check_gaps.py

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

def check_traffic_gaps(file_path: str):
    df = pd.read_parquet(file_path)
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows.")
    
    # Sort by location, category, veh_class, dir, and dt
    df = df.sort_values(['location', 'category', 'veh_class', 'dir', 'dt'])
    
    # Calculate time difference between consecutive rows within the same groups
    df['dt_diff'] = df.groupby(['location', 'category', 'veh_class', 'dir'])['dt'].diff()
    
    # Get stats on the gaps
    gaps = df['dt_diff'].dropna()
    print("\nGap Statistics (Time Deltas):")
    print(gaps.describe())
    
    # Count large gaps (e.g., > 15 minutes if data is expected to be more frequent)
    # Let's see what the most common frequency is first.
    mode_diff = gaps.mode()[0]
    print(f"\nMost common gap (Mode): {mode_diff}")
    
    large_gaps = gaps[gaps > mode_diff]
    print(f"Number of gaps larger than mode: {len(large_gaps)} ({len(large_gaps)/len(gaps)*100:.2f}%)")
    
    print("\nSample of large gaps:")
    print(large_gaps.value_counts().head(10))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    check_traffic_gaps('data/traffic_20230108_20260108.parquet')



================================================
FILE: src/dataset.py
================================================
import torch
from torch.utils.data import Dataset, DataLoader
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import logging
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler

# Standard random seed initialization
np.random.seed(42)
torch.manual_seed(42)

logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format='%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s')
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class RealIoTDataset(Dataset):
    """
    Production PyTorch Dataset for the ResilientIoTPipeline.
    Loads real parquet data efficiently using memory-friendly time filters.
    Groups data by location, applies sliding window extraction, and injects NaN masking.
    """
    def __init__(self, 
                 parquet_path: str, 
                 start_date: str, 
                 end_date: str, 
                 seq_len: int = 12, 
                 mask_prob: float = 0.15,
                 target_veh_class: str = 'person',
                 target_dir: str = 'all'):
        
        logger.info(f"Loading data from {parquet_path} between {start_date} and {end_date}")
        
        # 1. Load Data with Time Filter Constraints
        start_dt = pd.Timestamp(start_date)
        end_dt = pd.Timestamp(end_date)
        
        df = pd.read_parquet(
            parquet_path,
            filters=[
                ('dt', '>=', start_dt), 
                ('dt', '<', end_dt)
            ],
            columns=[
                'dt', 'location', 'value', 'Humidity', 'Solar Radiation', 
                'nearby_poi_count', 'dist_to_nearest_intersection', 
                'commercial_density', 'is_school_zone', 'highway_proximity',
                'veh_class', 'dir'
            ]
        )
        
        if df.empty:
            raise ValueError(f"No data found for the specified date range: {start_date} to {end_date}")

        available_classes = sorted(df['veh_class'].dropna().astype(str).unique().tolist())
        available_dirs = sorted(df['dir'].dropna().astype(str).unique().tolist())
            
        # 2. Filter target stream to ensure unique (dt, location) indices
        df = df[df['veh_class'] == target_veh_class]
        if df.empty:
            raise ValueError(
                f"No rows after veh_class filter. Requested veh_class='{target_veh_class}' "
                f"for range {start_date} to {end_date}. Available veh_class values: {available_classes}"
            )
        
        if target_dir == 'all':
            # Group by dt and location, sum values, take first for weather/static features
            agg_funcs = {
                'value': 'sum',
                'Humidity': 'first',
                'Solar Radiation': 'first',
                'nearby_poi_count': 'first',
                'dist_to_nearest_intersection': 'first',
                'commercial_density': 'first',
                'is_school_zone': 'first',
                'highway_proximity': 'first'
            }
            df = df.groupby(['dt', 'location'], as_index=False).agg(agg_funcs)
        else:
            df = df[df['dir'] == target_dir]
            if df.empty:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"No rows after dir filter. Requested dir='{target_dir}' with veh_class='{target_veh_class}' "
                    f"for range {start_date} to {end_date}. Available dir values before filtering: {available_dirs}"
                )

        if df.empty:
            raise ValueError(
                f"No rows available after filtering and aggregation for range {start_date} to {end_date}, "
                f"veh_class='{target_veh_class}', dir='{target_dir}'."
            )
            
        # 3. Extract dimensions
        self.locations = sorted(df['location'].unique())
        self.times = sorted(df['dt'].unique())
        self.seq_len = seq_len
        self.mask_prob = mask_prob
        self.num_nodes = len(self.locations)
        
        logger.info(f"Loaded {len(self.times)} timesteps across {self.num_nodes} locations for class={target_veh_class}, dir={target_dir}.")
        
        # 4. Pivot for Sliding Window Logic
        df = df.set_index(['dt', 'location'])
        
        # Dynamic features
        dyn_df = df[['value', 'Humidity', 'Solar Radiation']].unstack('location')
        locations_idx = pd.Index(self.locations, name='location')
        
        val_arr = dyn_df['value'].reindex(columns=locations_idx).fillna(0.0).values
        hum_arr = dyn_df['Humidity'].reindex(columns=locations_idx).fillna(0.0).values
        sol_arr = dyn_df['Solar Radiation'].reindex(columns=locations_idx).fillna(0.0).values

        # Preserve a raw, unscaled copy of traffic counts BEFORE any scaling.
        # ZINB NLL requires non-negative counts as targets; StandardScaler would
        # convert these to signed floats and corrupt the loss domain.
        self.unscaled_traffic = val_arr.copy()  # [num_times, num_nodes]

        # [num_times, num_nodes, 3]
        self.dynamic_data = np.stack([val_arr, hum_arr, sol_arr], axis=-1)
        self.time_series_dim = self.dynamic_data.shape[-1]

        # Scale dynamic features across the full dataset so the model receives
        # zero-mean, unit-variance inputs — critical for stable gradient flow.
        num_times, num_nodes_d, n_dyn_features = self.dynamic_data.shape
        dyn_flat = self.dynamic_data.reshape(-1, n_dyn_features)
        self.scaler = StandardScaler()
        dyn_scaled = self.scaler.fit_transform(dyn_flat)
        self.dynamic_data = dyn_scaled.reshape(num_times, num_nodes_d, n_dyn_features)
        logger.info(f"Dynamic features scaled: mean≈0, std≈1 over {num_times * num_nodes_d} samples.")

        # Static features
        stat_cols = [
            'nearby_poi_count', 
            'dist_to_nearest_intersection', 
            'commercial_density', 
            'is_school_zone', 
            'highway_proximity'
        ]
        stat_df = df[stat_cols].groupby('location').first()
        stat_df['is_school_zone'] = stat_df['is_school_zone'].astype(float).fillna(0.0)
        stat_df = stat_df.reindex(locations_idx).fillna(0.0)

        # Scale static features so DynamicGraphLearner receives normalised coordinates/metadata.
        stat_np = stat_df.values  # [num_nodes, static_feat_dim]
        self.static_scaler = StandardScaler()
        stat_np_scaled = self.static_scaler.fit_transform(stat_np)
        self.static_features = torch.tensor(stat_np_scaled, dtype=torch.float32)
        self.static_feat_dim = self.static_features.shape[-1]
        logger.info(f"Static features scaled: mean≈0, std≈1 over {stat_np.shape[0]} nodes.")
        
        logger.info(f"Feature dimensions established. Static: {self.static_feat_dim}, Dynamic: {self.time_series_dim}")

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return max(0, len(self.times) - self.seq_len)

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict:
        # Extract sliding window
        # Dynamic: [seq_len, num_nodes, D] -> permute -> [num_nodes, seq_len, D]
        dyn_slice = self.dynamic_data[idx : idx + self.seq_len]
        dyn_slice = np.transpose(dyn_slice, (1, 0, 2))
        
        dyn_tensor = torch.tensor(dyn_slice, dtype=torch.float32)

        # Forecast target: pull from unscaled traffic counts, not scaled dynamic_data.
        # clamp(min=0) is a hard tripwire — raw counts must never be negative,
        # but guards against any residual float precision artifacts from the parquet.
        target = self.unscaled_traffic[idx + self.seq_len]  # [num_nodes]
        target_tensor = torch.tensor(target, dtype=torch.float32).clamp(min=0)
        
        # 4. Dynamic Masking logic (~15% artificial NaN injection)
        node_mask = torch.rand_like(dyn_tensor) < self.mask_prob
        target_impute = dyn_tensor.clone()
        dyn_masked = dyn_tensor.clone()
        dyn_masked[node_mask] = float('nan')

        return {
            'static_features': self.static_features,
            'dynamic_features': dyn_masked,
            'target_impute': target_impute,
            'target_forecast': target_tensor,
            'node_mask': node_mask
        }

def custom_collate_real(batch_list):
    """Collate function to maintain node structural dimensions."""
    return {
        'static_features': batch_list[0]['static_features'], # Static across batch
        'dynamic_features': torch.stack([b['dynamic_features'] for b in batch_list]),
        'target_impute': torch.stack([b['target_impute'] for b in batch_list]),
        'target_forecast': torch.stack([b['target_forecast'] for b in batch_list]),
        'node_mask': torch.stack([b['node_mask'] for b in batch_list])
    }



================================================
FILE: src/decoders.py
================================================
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from typing import Tuple, Optional

class DecoupledDecoders(nn.Module):
    """
    DecoupledDecoders: Multi-task heads for imputation and uncertainty-aware forecasting.
    """
    def __init__(self, hidden_dim: int, time_series_dim: int, dropout: float = 0.1, zinb_eps: float = 1e-6):
        super(DecoupledDecoders, self).__init__()

        # Reproducibility
        torch.manual_seed(42)

        self.hidden_dim = hidden_dim
        self.time_series_dim = time_series_dim
        self.zinb_eps = zinb_eps

        # Imputation Decoder: Reconstructs the entire sequence.
        # Deepened to a 2-layer MLP to give the model enough capacity to recover
        # masked sensor readings from the latent graph representation.
        self.impute_layer = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim * 2),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Dropout(dropout),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim * 2, time_series_dim)
        )

        # Forecasting Decoder: Predicts ZINB parameters (mu, theta, pi).
        # Deepened to match imputation head capacity and reduce gradient starvation.
        self.forecast_layer = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim * 2),
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Dropout(dropout),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim * 2, 3)
        )

    def forward(self, latent_embeddings: torch.Tensor) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, list]:
        """
        Args:
            latent_embeddings: [batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, hidden_dim]
        Returns:
            imputed_features: [batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, time_series_dim]
            mu: [batch_size, num_nodes]
            theta: [batch_size, num_nodes]
            pi: [batch_size, num_nodes]
            tripwire_logs: list of dicts {'node_id', 'event_type'}
        """
        assert latent_embeddings.dim() == 4, f"Expected 4D embeddings, got {latent_embeddings.shape}"
        batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, hidden_dim = latent_embeddings.shape

        # 1. Imputation (across all time steps)
        imputed_features = self.impute_layer(latent_embeddings)

        # 2. Forecasting (using the final time step's embedding)
        last_step_embedding = latent_embeddings[:, :, -1, :] # [batch_size, num_nodes, hidden_dim]
        forecast_logits = self.forecast_layer(last_step_embedding) # [batch_size, num_nodes, 3]

        # Split raw logits
        mu_logits = forecast_logits[:, :, 0]
        theta_logits = forecast_logits[:, :, 1]
        pi_logits = forecast_logits[:, :, 2]

        # Apply ZINB constraints (Tripwires from NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md)
        pi = torch.sigmoid(pi_logits)
        mu = F.softplus(mu_logits) + self.zinb_eps
        theta = F.softplus(theta_logits) + self.zinb_eps

        # Detect Offesive Programming safeguard activations
        tripwire_logs = []
        pi_clamp = (pi < self.zinb_eps * 10) | (pi > 1.0 - self.zinb_eps * 10)
        mu_clamp = (F.softplus(mu_logits) < self.zinb_eps * 10)
        theta_clamp = (F.softplus(theta_logits) < self.zinb_eps * 10)

        for b in range(batch_size):
            for n in range(num_nodes):
                if pi_clamp[b, n]:
                    tripwire_logs.append({'node_id': n, 'event_type': 'PI_CLAMP'})
                if mu_clamp[b, n]:
                    tripwire_logs.append({'node_id': n, 'event_type': 'MU_CLAMP'})
                if theta_clamp[b, n]:
                    tripwire_logs.append({'node_id': n, 'event_type': 'THETA_CLAMP'})

        return imputed_features, mu, theta, pi, tripwire_logs

def compute_zinb_nll(y_true: torch.Tensor, mu: torch.Tensor, theta: torch.Tensor, pi: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """
    Negative Log-Likelihood for Zero-Inflated Negative Binomial Distribution.

    Formula:
    If y=0: P(y) = pi + (1-pi) * (theta / (theta + mu))^theta
    If y>0: P(y) = (1-pi) * [Gamma(y+theta)/(y!Gamma(theta))] * (theta/(theta+mu))^theta * (mu/(theta+mu))^y
    """
    eps = 1e-6
    y_true = y_true.float()

    # Clamp log operations to eps
    def safe_log(x):
        return torch.log(x.clamp(min=eps))

    # NB log-likelihood components
    # log(theta / (theta + mu))
    log_theta_mu_ratio = safe_log(theta) - safe_log(theta + mu)
    # log(mu / (theta + mu))
    log_mu_mu_ratio = safe_log(mu) - safe_log(theta + mu)

    # Case y > 0
    # log P(y|NB) = logGamma(y+theta) - logGamma(y+1) - logGamma(theta) + theta*log(theta/(theta+mu)) + y*log(mu/(theta+mu))
    ll_nb = (torch.lgamma(y_true + theta)
             - torch.lgamma(y_true + 1)
             - torch.lgamma(theta)
             + theta * log_theta_mu_ratio
             + y_true * log_mu_mu_ratio)

    # Case y = 0
    # P(y=0|ZINB) = pi + (1-pi) * exp(theta * log(theta/(theta+mu)))
    # For numerical stability, we use log-sum-exp or handle it carefully
    # log_p_zero_nb = theta * log_theta_mu_ratio
    ll_zero = safe_log(pi + (1 - pi) * torch.exp(theta * log_theta_mu_ratio))

    # Case y > 0 (ZINB)
    # log P(y|ZINB) = log(1-pi) + ll_nb
    ll_nonzero = safe_log(1 - pi) + ll_nb

    # Combine cases
    result = torch.where(y_true < eps, ll_zero, ll_nonzero)

    return -torch.mean(result)

def compute_loss(imputed_features: torch.Tensor,
                 target_impute: torch.Tensor,
                 mu: torch.Tensor,
                 theta: torch.Tensor,
                 pi: torch.Tensor,
                 target_forecast: torch.Tensor,
                 w_impute: float = 1.0,
                 w_forecast: float = 1.0) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    """
    Joint Loss: MSE for Imputation + ZINB NLL for Forecasting.
    """
    # 1. Imputation Loss (MSE)
    # NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md: "strictly on the artificially masked nodes"
    # Note: In a real scenario, we'd use a mask here.
    loss_impute = F.mse_loss(imputed_features, target_impute)

    # 2. Forecasting Loss (ZINB NLL)
    loss_forecast = compute_zinb_nll(target_forecast, mu, theta, pi)

    # 3. Total Weighted Loss
    total_loss = (w_impute * loss_impute) + (w_forecast * loss_forecast)

    # --- Tripwires (Rule 5 & NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md) ---
    if torch.isnan(total_loss).any():
        # Print stats for debugging before crashing
        print(f"DEBUG: mu min={mu.min()}, max={mu.max()}")
        print(f"DEBUG: theta min={theta.min()}, max={theta.max()}")
        print(f"DEBUG: pi min={pi.min()}, max={pi.max()}")
        raise ValueError("CRITICAL FAILURE: Loss is NaN. Mathematical tripwire triggered.")

    return total_loss, loss_impute, loss_forecast

FILE: src/encoder.py
================================================
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch_geometric.nn import GATv2Conv
from typing import Optional, Tuple, Union

class MaskedSpatiotemporalEncoder(nn.Module):
    """
    MaskedSpatiotemporalEncoder: Processes dynamic IoT data using GATv2 and Transformers.
    
    Architecture:
    1. Spatial: GATv2Conv layers applied independently across each time step.
    2. Temporal: TransformerEncoder to capture dependencies across the sequence.
    """
    def __init__(self, 
                 num_nodes: int, 
                 seq_len: int, 
                 time_series_dim: int, 
                 hidden_dim: int = 64, 
                 num_heads: int = 4, 
                 num_layers: int = 2,
                 dropout: float = 0.1,
                 dim_feedforward: int = 256):
        super(MaskedSpatiotemporalEncoder, self).__init__()
        
        # Reproducibility at initialization
        torch.manual_seed(42)
        
        self.num_nodes = num_nodes
        self.seq_len = seq_len
        self.time_series_dim = time_series_dim
        self.hidden_dim = hidden_dim

        # 1. Spatial Processing: GATv2Conv
        # We use a single layer for simplicity as per common IoT patterns, 
        # or can be extended to multiple.
        self.gat = GATv2Conv(
            in_channels=time_series_dim,
            out_channels=hidden_dim // num_heads,
            heads=num_heads,
            dropout=dropout,
            edge_dim=1 # For edge_weight
        )

        # 2. Temporal Processing: Transformer
        encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
            d_model=hidden_dim,
            nhead=num_heads,
            dim_feedforward=dim_feedforward,
            dropout=dropout,
            batch_first=False # Standard Transformer [Seq, Batch, Dim]
        )
        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=num_layers)

    def forward(
        self,
        dynamic_features: torch.Tensor,
        edge_index: torch.Tensor,
        edge_weight: torch.Tensor,
        return_attention_weights: bool = False,
    ) -> Union[torch.Tensor, Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]]:
        """
        Args:
            dynamic_features: [batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, time_series_dim]
            edge_index: [2, E]
            edge_weight: [E]
            return_attention_weights: If True, also return the GAT attention
                coefficients alpha [batched_E, num_heads] for downstream analysis.
        Returns:
            latent_embeddings: [batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, hidden_dim]
            alpha (optional): [batched_E, num_heads] — only when return_attention_weights=True.
        """
        # --- Defensive Data Handling (Rule 2) ---
        assert dynamic_features.dim() == 4, f"Expected 4D features, got {dynamic_features.shape}"
        batch_size, num_nodes, seq_len, ts_dim = dynamic_features.shape
        assert num_nodes == self.num_nodes, f"Node mismatch: {num_nodes} != {self.num_nodes}"
        assert seq_len == self.seq_len, f"Seq mismatch: {seq_len} != {self.seq_len}"
        assert ts_dim == self.time_series_dim, f"Feature mismatch: {ts_dim} != {self.time_series_dim}"

        # --- Spatial Processing ---
        # Reshape to [B, T, N, D] then to [B*T*N, D]
        # We want to apply GAT to each (batch, time_step) independently.
        x = dynamic_features.permute(0, 2, 1, 3).contiguous() # [B, T, N, D]
        x = x.view(batch_size * seq_len * num_nodes, ts_dim)

        # Construct batched edge_index
        # Original edge_index is [2, E] for num_nodes.
        # We need to repeat it batch_size * seq_len times with offsets.
        num_graphs = batch_size * seq_len
        E = edge_index.shape[1]
        
        # [2, E * num_graphs]
        batched_edge_index = edge_index.repeat(1, num_graphs)
        offsets = torch.arange(num_graphs, device=edge_index.device).view(-1, 1) * num_nodes
        # offsets shape: [num_graphs, 1]. Repeat for each edge in the graph.
        offsets = offsets.repeat(1, E).view(-1) # [num_graphs * E]
        batched_edge_index = batched_edge_index + offsets
        
        # edge_weight also needs repeating
        batched_edge_weight = edge_weight.repeat(num_graphs)

        # Apply GAT — optionally collect attention coefficients for analysis.
        if return_attention_weights:
            # PyG returns (out, (edge_index_used, alpha)) when return_attention_weights=True
            x_spatial, (_, alpha) = self.gat(
                x, batched_edge_index,
                edge_attr=batched_edge_weight,
                return_attention_weights=True,
            )
        else:
            x_spatial = self.gat(x, batched_edge_index, edge_attr=batched_edge_weight)
            alpha = None

        # --- Temporal Processing ---
        # Reshape back to [B, T, N, H]
        x_temporal = x_spatial.view(batch_size, seq_len, num_nodes, self.hidden_dim)
        
        # Transformer expects [Seq, Batch, Dim]
        # We treat each node in each batch as an independent sequence.
        # Batch dimension for Transformer: B * N
        # Seq dimension: T
        x_temporal = x_temporal.permute(1, 0, 2, 3).contiguous() # [T, B, N, H]
        x_temporal = x_temporal.view(seq_len, batch_size * num_nodes, self.hidden_dim)

        # Pass through Transformer
        # x_out: [T, B * N, H]
        x_out = self.transformer(x_temporal)

        # Reshape back to [B, N, T, H]
        x_out = x_out.view(seq_len, batch_size, num_nodes, self.hidden_dim)
        x_out = x_out.permute(1, 2, 0, 3).contiguous() # [B, N, T, H]

        # --- Tripwires (Rule 5) ---
        if torch.isnan(x_out).any():
            raise ValueError("NaN detected in MaskedSpatiotemporalEncoder output!")

        if return_attention_weights:
            return x_out, alpha
        return x_out

FILE: src/export_ui_payload.py
