import os
import torch
import pandas as pd
from src.dataset import RealIoTDataset

def test_dataset_places_enrichment():
    parquet_path = "data/processed/master_dataset_15T.parquet"
    
    if not os.path.exists(parquet_path):
        print(f"Parquet file {parquet_path} not found. Skipping test.")
        return

    # 1. Instantiate dataset with real parquet file
    # We use a small date range for the smoke test
    try:
        dataset = RealIoTDataset(
            parquet_path=parquet_path,
            start_date="2024-04-02",
            end_date="2024-04-03",
            seq_len=12,
            mask_prob=0.15,
            target_veh_class="person",
            target_dir="all"
        )
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Failed to load dataset: {e}")
        return
    
    # 2. Assert correct static feature dimensionality
    print(f"Loaded Static Feature Shape: {dataset.static_features.shape}")
    print(f"Dataset static_feat_dim: {dataset.static_feat_dim}")
    
    # This will fail initially as expected (it should be 2 currently)
    assert dataset.static_feat_dim == 5, f"Expected 5 features, got {dataset.static_feat_dim}"
    assert dataset.static_features.shape == (dataset.num_nodes, 5), "Static features shape mismatch!"
    
    # 3. Assert correct normalization (mean close to 0, std close to 1)
    mean = dataset.static_features.mean(dim=0)
    std = dataset.static_features.std(dim=0)
    print(f"Static Feature Means: {mean.tolist()}")
    print(f"Static Feature StdDevs: {std.tolist()}")
    
    assert torch.allclose(mean, torch.zeros_like(mean), atol=1e-1), "Static features mean is not normalized!"
    
    # 4. Assert absence of NaNs / Infs
    assert not torch.isnan(dataset.static_features).any(), "Found NaNs in static features!"
    assert not torch.isinf(dataset.static_features).any(), "Found Infs in static features!"
    
    print("✓ Dataset Smoke Test Passed Successfully!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_dataset_places_enrichment()

# Antigravity Agent Registry

## The Interrogator (@interrogator)

### SYSTEM ROLE: INTERROGATOR

You are the "Interrogator," a specialized scout agent operating on the **local host system**. Your primary function is to investigate the codebase, extract relevant information, and provide concise, accurate summaries and context to the "Architect" agent. You are the eyes and ears of the operation.

### 1. CAPABILITIES

- You have full read access to the entire repository, including src/, tests/, frontend/src/, and all configuration files.
- You are equipped with the Filesystem MCP server, enabling you to use tools like read_file, search_files, and list_directory.
- You have access to Weights & Biases (W&B) logs to analyze model performance and experiment metrics.

### 2. RULES OF ENGAGEMENT

1. **Do Not Modify Code:** Your role is strictly investigative. You must never attempt to modify, delete, or create functional files in the repository.
2. **Output to Context File (Strict):** You must NEVER create an implementation plan. Your sole objective is to write the gathered context, relevant file paths, high-level logic summaries, and Abstract Syntax Trees (ASTs) to `.agents/plans/context_for_architect.md`. You must ALWAYS cleanly overwrite this file, never append.
3. **Summarization over Raw Code:** Unless explicitly instructed otherwise, provide high-level logic summaries or Abstract Syntax Trees (ASTs) rather than raw code dumps. Focus on explaining what the code does and how it interacts with other components.
4. **Identify Tripwires:** Actively search for potential issues, violations of the North Star guidelines, or missing safeguards (e.g., missing assertions, silently dropped data, or unhandled NaN values). Flag these immediately.
5. **Alert on Defensive Code (CRITICAL):** If you find any remotely defensively written code (e.g., silent error handling, broad except blocks, silent data dropping) that has snuck into the codebase, you MUST alert the user immediately.
6. **Analyze W&B Logs:** When asked about model performance, retrieve and synthesize relevant data from W&B logs to provide insights into training progress, metrics, and hyperparameter sweeps.
7. **Contextualize Findings:** Always provide context for your findings. Explain why a particular piece of code or log entry is relevant to the Architect's inquiry.

## The Architect (@architect)

### SYSTEM ROLE: ARCHITECT

You are the "Architect," a senior Machine Learning Engineer and system designer operating on the **local host system**. Your primary function is to analyze the information provided by the Interrogator and formulate robust, scalable, and mathematically sound architectural solutions that adhere strictly to the project's North Star guidelines.

### 1. CAPABILITIES

- You do not have direct access to the codebase. You rely entirely on the information and summaries provided by the Interrogator via `.agents/plans/context_for_architect.md`.
- You have deep knowledge of the project's North Star documents: NSTAR_MLOPS.md, NSTAR_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.md, NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md, and NSTAR_PHASES.md.
- You understand the "Decoupled Parallelism" strategy and the importance of the ui_payload.json static data bridge.

### 2. RULES OF ENGAGEMENT

1. **Enforce the North Stars:** Your designs must strictly align with the principles outlined in the North Star documents. Prioritize Offensive Programming (fail fast, assert everything) and Decoupled Systems.
2. **Output to Plan File (Strict):** Your role is to design and plan, not to write execution code. You will generate a high-level, step-by-step architectural design and save it to `.agents/plans/architect_plan.md`. You must ALWAYS cleanly overwrite this file.
3. **Demand Evidence:** If the Interrogator provides insufficient information, demand further investigation. Do not make assumptions about the codebase.
4. **Mandate "Desk Checks":** When designing mathematical logic, metrics, or loss functions, mandate a "Desk Check" in your implementation plan to ensure theoretical correctness before the Implementer writes code.
5. **Update the Ledger:** Every implementation prompt you generate must include a strict instruction for the implementing agent to update EXPERIMENTS.md with the task's outcome, branch name, and date.

## The Implementer (@implementer)

### SYSTEM ROLE: SENIOR MACHINE LEARNING ENGINEER

You are an autonomous Machine Learning Agent operating **within a containerized Linux environment**. You have read/write access to the local filesystem. Your objective is to build, test, and debug machine learning pipelines securely and reproducibly based on the Architect's plan.

### 1. RULES OF ENGAGEMENT

- **Plan First (Strict):** Do not write execution code immediately upon receiving a complex request. You must read `.agents/plans/architect_plan.md`. Based on this, you will generate a detailed, file-by-file execution plan and save it to `.agents/plans/implementation_plan.md` (cleanly overwriting it).
- **Wait for Approval:** You MUST explicitly wait for the USER to approve `implementation_plan.md` before writing any functional code.
- **Safety First (Git):** Before making any multi-file changes or refactoring working code, you MUST execute `git add .` and `git commit -m "Auto-commit before [Task Name]"` to ensure the user has a rollback point.
- **Autonomy:** You have permission to run standard bash commands (e.g., `ls`, `cat`, `python`) to explore the environment and test your code. However, you are strictly forbidden from running `pip install` or `npm install` directly in your default agent shell. You must ask for permission before deleting entire directories or dropping databases. All dependency management must follow the Strict Container Isolation paradigm.

### 2. MACHINE LEARNING STANDARDS

- **Reproducibility:** Every script must include standard random seed initialization for numpy, pandas, scikit-learn, and torch/tensorflow at the very top of the execution block.
- **Defensive Data Handling:** Before passing data into a model, you must explicitly print/log the tensor or dataframe shapes (e.g., `X.shape`, `y.shape`). If there is a mismatch, catch it and fix the reshaping logic before proceeding.
- **Directory Structure:** Adhere strictly to the workspace structure.
  - Raw data is in `./data/`. Treat this as READ-ONLY.
  - Python scripts go in `./src/`.
  - Trained model artifacts (`.pkl`, `.pt`, `.h5`) must be saved to `./models/`.
- **Code Quality:** Write modular, functional Python (3.11+). Avoid massive, monolithic scripts. Use Python type hints (`def process_data(df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:`) for all function definitions. Include concise docstrings explaining the input/output of complex data transformations. Favor logging (`import logging`) over standard `print()` statements for tracking training epochs and data pipeline progress.

### 3. ERROR RESOLUTION

- If a Python script throws an error during your testing, do not immediately ask the user for help.
- Read the traceback, identify the exact line of failure (often a tensor dimension mismatch, a missing column, or a NaN value), apply a fix, and rerun the script.
- Only present the final, working output to the user, summarizing the errors you encountered and how you resolved them.
- Store tests and debugging scripts in a separate `./tests/` directory. Do not clutter the main `./src/` directory with temporary code.

### 4. OFFENSIVE PROGRAMMING PARADIGM (FAIL FAST / FAIL HARD)

- You are writing prototype ML code. Do NOT write defensive code that silently handles errors, coerces data types, or suppresses exceptions.
- **Assert Everything:** Use `assert` statements aggressively at the top of functions to validate matrix shapes, exact data types, and expected column names.
- **Let it Burn:** Never use a bare `except:` block. If an unexpected error occurs, the script must crash immediately and print the full traceback so the error can be diagnosed.
- **Tripwires:** If a tensor operation or pandas transformation might introduce NaNs or infinites, add an explicit check immediately after the operation to crash the script if they are detected.
- **No Silent Drops:** Do not use `.dropna()` or `.fillna()` silently unless explicitly instructed as part of the data cleaning plan.
- **Alert on Defensive Code (CRITICAL):** If you find any remotely defensively written code (e.g., silent error handling, broad except blocks, silent data dropping) that has snuck into the codebase, you MUST alert the user immediately.

### 5. THE EXPERIMENT LEDGER (DOCUMENTATION MANDATE)

- You are required to maintain a strict log of all major architectural changes and model training runs in `EXPERIMENTS.md`.
- **Branching Protocol:** Whenever you are instructed to build a new model or start a major feature, you must first ask the user if they have created a new Git branch.
- **Logging Protocol:** Upon completing a training run, building a new pipeline component, or hitting a dead end, you MUST append a new row to the table in `EXPERIMENTS.md`.
- The row must include the current date, the active Git branch name, a summary of the exact prompt/goal that initiated the work, and the mathematical or functional outcome.
- A task is only considered 'Done' once the outcome has been recorded in EXPERIMENTS.md.
- You are strictly forbidden from saying "The task is complete" or "I have finished" in your response unless you have already updated the ledger in the same turn.

### 6. THE NORTH STARS (NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md, NSTAR_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE, NSTAR_MLOPS.md, & NSTAR_PHASES.md)

- The Blueprints: You have three global design documents.
  1. Use `NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md` for any PyTorch module, tensor, or mathematical logic.
  2. Use `NSTAR_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.md` for any Next.js, Deck.gl, or geospatial UI logic.
  3. Use `NSTAR_MLOPS.md` for any task involving training loops, Weights & Biases, Optuna, or Docker execution.
  4. Use `NSTAR_PHASES.md` as your temporal anchor. You must strictly adhere to the constraints of the current Phase (e.g., do not write live API fetching logic if the project is currently in the Static Payload phase).
- Piecewise Execution: DO NOT attempt to build the entire system at once. You must only build the specific component requested in the user's prompt.
- Strict Adherence: The constraints listed in NSTAR_BACKEND_ARCHITECTURE.md (especially regarding loss functions, tripwires, and data masking) are absolute. Do not substitute them with standard generic functions.

### 7. THE DESK CHECK MANDATE

- When asked to review mathematical logic, metrics, or loss functions, you are forbidden from simply saying "the logic looks correct." You MUST run a simulated "Desk Check." You must create a dummy scenario with extreme, unbalanced numbers (e.g., a batch of 100 vs a batch of 1, or an input tensor of exactly 0.0), trace the exact mathematical execution of the code, and prove that the final output matches the theoretical target.

### 8. FILE ACCESS PROTOCOL (STRICT CONTEXT LIMITS)

- You are strictly forbidden from reading, listing (ls), or attempting to parse the contents of the following directories and file types: `wandb/`, `logs/`, `models/`, `.next/`, `.pytest_cache/`, `**pycache**/`, `_.parquet`, and `_.pt`.
- CRITICAL DATA BAN: The entire `data/` directory and frontend/public/data/ directory are absolutely off-limits. You must NEVER read ui_payload.json. It is a massive file that will instantly max out your context window. If you need to know the structure of the data, refer ONLY to the TypeScript interfaces in `frontend/src/types/payload.ts` or write a Python script to print df.head(). Do not pipe raw data into this chat.

### 9. STRICT CONTAINER ISOLATION PARADIGM

- This project operates under a strict "Zero Host Download" rule. Your workspace directory on the host machine must remain clean.
- No Local Installs: You must NEVER run npm install or pip install directly on the host machine. All dependencies must be installed inside their respective Docker containers.
- Node Modules Quarantine: Frontend dependencies are managed via Docker anonymous volumes. If you need to install a new frontend package (e.g., `npm install d3`), you must execute it inside the running frontend container using `docker compose exec [service_name] npm install d3`.
- Verification: Do not attempt to verify installations by looking for a node_modules folder in the host's `frontend/` directory. It should not exist there. Verify by running commands inside the container.
- You are strictly forbidden from creating Python virtual environments (e.g., `python -m venv`) inside the /workspace directory. Python dependencies must be managed globally within the container's native environment. If you require a new Python package, you must install it by executing `docker compose exec [service_name] pip install <package>` and immediately adhere to the Sync or Sink rule.

# Architect Plan: Removing Places Template and Code Cleanup

This document outlines the high-level, step-by-step architectural design to guide the `@implementer` in removing the `places_template` path dependency and cleaning up the codebase, specifically eliminating defensive programming try-except blocks in favor of the **Offensive Programming (Fail Fast)** paradigm.

---

## 1. High-Level Design Principles

1.  **Enforce the North Star Guidelines (Offensive Programming):**
    *   **Fail Fast / Fail Hard / Let it Burn:** Eliminate defensive `try-except` blocks that catch general exceptions or specific errors to fall back silently. If the environment setup or external resources fail (e.g. missing API keys, connection issues, parse errors), allow the pipeline to crash immediately with a clear traceback.
    *   **Assert Inputs:** Validate all incoming variables (such as checking coordinates are valid numbers and features are integers) using defensive assertions.
2.  **Decoupled Systems:**
    *   Completely eliminate the redundant, untracked `google_places_template.csv`.
    *   Migrate all necessary semantic tags (specifically `nearby_poi_count`) to load directly from the Google Places API cache (`places_cache.json` / `places_cache_test.json`), fully decoupling the system from dynamic CSV structures.

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Architectural Tasks

### Task 1: Refactor `load_semantic_tags` in `src/export_ui_payload.py`
*   **Remove Default Argument:** Drop the `template_path: str = PLACES_TEMPLATE_PATH` argument from the signature of `load_semantic_tags`.
*   **Eliminate Defensive Cache Fallback:**
    *   Remove the `try/except (ValueError, RuntimeError) as e` block around `StaticFeatureLoader` initialization. Allow any initialization error to crash the script immediately.
*   **Eliminate Defensive Simulated Node Live Query Error Swallowing:**
    *   Remove the inner `try/except Exception as ex` block around simulated node live fetching. If the live query fails, the script must fail hard.
*   **Integrate POI Density Tags directly from Cache (Source 1):**
    *   Within the cache loading iteration, retrieve `nearby_poi_count` from `feats` and append the respective `high_poi_density`, `medium_poi_density`, or `low_poi_density` tag.
*   **Completely Drop Source 2 (CSV Template):**
    *   Delete the entire logic block parsing the CSV template.
    *   Discard the unused synthetic `zone_type` tags.
*   **Clean Up Unused Import:**
    *   Delete `import csv` on line 23 since CSV parsing is no longer used in this file.

### Task 2: Refactor Configuration Loading in `src/export_ui_payload.py`
*   **Eliminate Silent Config Fallback:**
    *   Remove the `try/except Exception as e` around co-located configuration yaml reading (`config.yaml`). If the file exists but has syntax or file errors, the script must crash immediately.

### Task 3: Simplify `src/smoke_test.py`
*   **Eliminate Redundant CSV Creation:**
    *   Remove Section 2 (`Populate the Google Places Template`) entirely.
    *   The smoke test no longer needs to create or write the `google_places_template.csv` file since the PreprocessingPipeline and StaticFeatureLoader do not consume it.

---

## 3. Mandated "Desk Checks"

The `@implementer` must perform the following "Desk Checks" before submitting any functional code to verify correctness:

### A. Semantic Tag Extraction Desk Check
Simulate the output of `load_semantic_tags` for the following mock data cases:
1.  **Case 1 (Missing Key):** A cached feature dict `{"commercial_density": 20, "is_school_zone": true}` (missing `highway_proximity` and `nearby_poi_count`).
    *   *Expected behavior:* `feats.get("nearby_poi_count", 0)` should default to `0` and not raise `KeyError`. The tags should evaluate to `["high_commercial_density", "school_zone"]`.
2.  **Case 2 (Boundary Thresholds):**
    *   `commercial_density = 15` -> Expected tag: `"high_commercial_density"`
    *   `commercial_density = 5` -> Expected tag: `"medium_commercial_density"`
    *   `commercial_density = 4` -> Expected tag: `"low_commercial_density"`
    *   `nearby_poi_count = 20` -> Expected tag: `"high_poi_density"`
    *   `nearby_poi_count = 8` -> Expected tag: `"medium_poi_density"`
    *   `nearby_poi_count = 1` -> Expected tag: `"low_poi_density"`
    *   `nearby_poi_count = 0` -> Expected tag: None.

### B. Exception Bubbling Check
Trace execution flow when `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` is not present in `.env`:
*   *Expected behavior:* `StaticFeatureLoader` initialization throws `ValueError("GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY not found in environment.")`. The exception must bubble up and crash `export_ui_payload.py` with a full traceback, instead of catching and falling back to direct JSON read.

---

## 4. Documentation & Verification Mandate

1.  **Verification Run:** The `@implementer` must run `python src/smoke_test.py` and `python src/export_ui_payload.py` inside the container to ensure no runtime syntax errors exist and compilation works correctly.
2.  **Update the Ledger (CRITICAL):**
    The `@implementer` is strictly instructed to append a new entry to the `EXPERIMENTS.md` ledger table upon successful completion of the changes. The entry must record:
    *   **Date:** Current date.
    *   **Branch:** The active git branch.
    *   **Prompt/Goal:** "Remove places template path dependency and clean up code in export_ui_payload.py and smoke_test.py, eliminating defensive programming try-except blocks."
    *   **Mathematical/Functional Outcome:** Summary of cleaned-up code, successful removal of `google_places_template.csv`, and validation via smoke test execution.

# Context for Architect: Removing Places Template and Code Cleanup

This document contains the gathered codebase context, relevant file paths, high-level logic summaries, and ASTs to guide the `@architect` in formulating a robust plan for removing the `places_template` path dependency and eliminating defensive programming antipatterns in [export_ui_payload.py](file:///Users/administrator/Code/ml-agent-workspace/src/export_ui_payload.py).

---

## 1. Identified References & Antipatterns

### A. places_template References
*   **File:** [export_ui_payload.py](file:///Users/administrator/Code/ml-agent-workspace/src/export_ui_payload.py)
    *   **Line 23:** `import csv`
    *   **Line 66:** `# PLACES_TEMPLATE_PATH = "data/external/google_places_template.csv"`
    *   **Lines 223–227:** `load_semantic_tags` function signature utilizes `PLACES_TEMPLATE_PATH`.
    *   **Lines 283–305:** Code block parsing the CSV template to assign `zone_type` and `nearby_poi_count` tags (Source 2).
    *   **Line 1025:** Comment referencing `# 8. Load semantic tags from Places cache + template CSV`.
*   **File:** [smoke_test.py](file:///Users/administrator/Code/ml-agent-workspace/src/smoke_test.py)
    *   **Lines 38–46:** Mock metadata generation and file export block writing `google_places_template.csv`.

### B. Defensive try-except Antipatterns in `export_ui_payload.py`

> [!CRITICAL]
> **Defensive Coding Violations (Offensive Programming Paradigm):**
> Multiple try-except blocks in `export_ui_payload.py` catch general exceptions or specific errors to fall back silently or log a warning rather than letting the script crash. Under the project's **Fail Fast / Fail Hard** mandate, these must be eliminated.

1.  **Silent Fallback to Cache Manual Read (Lines 255-261):**
    ```python
    except (ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
        logger.warning(f"StaticFeatureLoader unavailable: {e}. Falling back to manual cache read.")
        cache = {}
        if Path(places_cache_path).exists():
            with open(places_cache_path) as f:
                cache = json.load(f)
    ```
    *   **Why it's an antipattern:** If `StaticFeatureLoader` fails to initialize (e.g. because `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` is missing or invalid), catching this exception and falling back to a direct file read hides critical configuration issues in development and production environments.
    *   **Correction:** Remove the entire `try/except` block. Allow `StaticFeatureLoader` initialization to raise exceptions directly, causing the script to crash immediately.

2.  **Silent Ignored Exception in Simulated Node Live Fetch (Lines 247-252):**
    ```python
    try:
        # Note: Using private method as requested in Task 3.2
        loader.cache[loc] = loader._fetch_location_data(meta["lat"], meta["lon"])
        loader._save_cache()
    except Exception as ex:
        logger.error(f"Failed to fetch data for simulated node {loc}: {ex}")
    ```
    *   **Why it's an antipattern:** If fetching live data from Google Places API fails during generation (due to network drops, quota, or authentication failures), it logs an error but silently continues with an incomplete cache/metadata.
    *   **Correction:** Remove the `try/except` block. If the live query fails, the script must fail hard so that engineers are immediately alerted of API/network failures.

3.  **Silent Bare Exception in W&B Metadata Retrieval (Lines 141-145):**
    ```python
    try:
        if artifact.producer:
            wandb_run_id = artifact.producer.id
    except Exception:
        pass
    ```
    *   **Why it's an antipattern:** Silently suppresses all exceptions with a bare `except Exception: pass`.
    *   **Correction:** While obtaining optional metadata, if it fails, it should either raise or be handled explicitly, rather than using bare swallow patterns.

4.  **Silent Config Parsing Fallback (Lines 749-750):**
    ```python
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to parse co-located configuration: {e}. Falling back to default architecture...")
    ```
    *   **Why it's an antipattern:** Silently absorbs configuration parsing errors and substitutes defaults, increasing the risk of subtle shape mismatches during downstream loading.
    *   **Correction:** Crash immediately on failing to parse configuration files.

---

## 2. Decoupling & Cleanup Strategy

### A. Semantic Tag Extraction Migration
*   **Remove CSV template dependency entirely:** `google_places_template.csv` is redundant.
*   **Migrate POI Density Tags:** Transfer POI density tag assignment (`high_poi_density`, `medium_poi_density`, `low_poi_density`) to use the cached value (`nearby_poi_count` from `places_cache.json`) inside the cache loop.
*   **Discard `zone_type` Tags:** Since `zone_type` is a mock field only present in the template CSV, discontinue these tags.

### B. Code Execution Without Try-Except Block
By removing the `try/except` block in `load_semantic_tags`, the code becomes incredibly clean and strictly adheres to the offensive programming guidelines:

```python
def load_semantic_tags(
    nodes_meta: List[Dict],
    places_cache_path: str = PLACES_CACHE_PATH,
) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    """
    Build a {location_name: [tag, ...]} mapping from the Places cache.

    Source:
      1. StaticFeatureLoader (Places API) — live API features for simulated
         nodes + cached features for real nodes.
    """
    tags: Dict[str, List[str]] = {m["location_name"]: [] for m in nodes_meta}

    # Initialize loader; will raise ValueError/RuntimeError and crash on auth/quota/missing key issues
    loader = StaticFeatureLoader(cache_path=places_cache_path)

    # Live Query for simulated nodes missing from cache
    for meta in nodes_meta:
        loc = meta["location_name"]
        if meta["is_simulated"] and loc not in loader.cache:
            logger.info(f"Fetching live data for simulated node: {loc}")
            # Note: Using private method as requested in Task 3.2
            loader.cache[loc] = loader._fetch_location_data(meta["lat"], meta["lon"])
            loader._save_cache()

    cache = loader.cache
    
    # Process features and append tags from Cache
    for loc, feats in cache.items():
        if loc not in tags:
            continue
        t = tags[loc]
        
        # Commercial density
        cd = int(feats.get("commercial_density", 0))
        if cd >= 15:
            t.append("high_commercial_density")
        elif cd >= 5:
            t.append("medium_commercial_density")
        elif cd > 0:
            t.append("low_commercial_density")
            
        # School zone
        if feats.get("is_school_zone"):
            t.append("school_zone")
            
        # Transit density
        hp = int(feats.get("highway_proximity", 0))
        if hp >= 15:
            t.append("high_transit_density")
        elif hp >= 5:
            t.append("medium_transit_density")
        elif hp > 0:
            t.append("low_transit_density")

        # POI density (Transferred from template CSV to live cache)
        poi = int(feats.get("nearby_poi_count", 0))
        if poi >= 20:
            t.append("high_poi_density")
        elif poi >= 8:
            t.append("medium_poi_density")
        elif poi >= 1:
            t.append("low_poi_density")

    return tags
```

---

### [MODIFY] [export_ui_payload.py](file:///Users/administrator/Code/ml-agent-workspace/src/export_ui_payload.py)

FILE: .agents/scripts/process_diagram.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import os
import sys
import json
from datetime import datetime

def main():
    # Read the JSON payload from standard input (piped from Antigravity)
    try:
        payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        print(f"Error: Invalid JSON input received. {e}", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)

    diagram_code = payload.get("diagram_code")
    human_instructions = payload.get("human_instructions")

    if not diagram_code or not human_instructions:
        print("Error: Missing 'diagram_code' or 'human_instructions' in payload.", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)

    # 1. Update the current Mermaid diagram file
    diagram_filename = "architecture_current.mmd"
    with open(diagram_filename, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(diagram_code)
    print(f"Successfully updated {diagram_filename}")

    # 2. Append to the Architecture Changelog
    changelog_filename = "ARCHITECTURE_CHANGELOG.md"

    # Create file with a header if it doesn't exist
    if not os.path.exists(changelog_filename):
        with open(changelog_filename, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write("# Architecture Evolution Changelog\n\n")

    current_time = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")

    changelog_entry = f"## Update: {current_time}\n\n{human_instructions}\n\n---\n\n"

    # Prepend or append? Usually, appending or writing to top is preferred.
    # Let's prepend right under the main header for easy reading.
    with open(changelog_filename, "r+", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        content = f.read()
        # Find the main header to place the new content right below it
        header_marker = "# Architecture Evolution Changelog\n\n"
        if content.startswith(header_marker):
            body = content[len(header_marker):]
            f.seek(len(header_marker))
            f.write(changelog_entry + body)
        else:
            # Fallback to append if header was modified
            f.seek(0, 2)
            f.write(changelog_entry)

    print(f"Successfully appended entry to {changelog_filename}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# Feature Development Pipeline

**Trigger:** When the user suggests a feature to work on, a bug to fix, or some refactoring.

**Steps:**

1. **Interrogation:** `@interrogator` investigates the codebase relevant to the user's request. It outputs all findings, file paths, and context to `.agents/plans/context_for_architect.md` (CLEAN overwrite). It does NOT create a plan.
2. **Architecture:** `@architect` reads `context_for_architect.md` and the `NSTAR_*.md` files. It outputs a high-level design plan to `.agents/plans/architect_plan.md` (CLEAN overwrite).
3. **Implementation Planning:** `@implementer` reads `architect_plan.md` and outputs a step-by-step execution plan (files to edit, code to write) to `.agents/plans/implementation_plan.md` (CLEAN overwrite).
4. **Approval:** Wait for the USER to explicitly approve `implementation_plan.md`.
5. **Execution:** `@implementer` writes the code, runs necessary tests via Docker, updates `EXPERIMENTS.md`, and commits the changes.



================================================
FILE: .claude/settings.local.json
================================================
